# PROGRESS.md — append-only build log

Format per entry: task id · what changed · how verified.

## Open questions

- OQ-1 (§14) Product name/domain: keeping the placeholder "BidRadar" everywhere; rename is a find/replace later.
- OQ-2 (§14) Internal tenant profile seed: seeding a placeholder internal tenant with empty profile; the owner fills UEI/NAICS/past performance via the wizard.
- OQ-3 (§14) SAM.gov key type: code assumes a single non-federal personal key per environment (`SAM_API_KEY`), quota-aware limiter defaults to 10 req/day headroom; a system account is a config change only.
- OQ-4 (§14) Indian state portals: Telangana, Karnataka, Maharashtra as proposed; IREPS/defproc shipped as documented stubs.
- OQ-5 (§14) SLED: no HigherGov licence assumed; SLED ships as a documented adapter stub with the paid-feed adapter interface.
- OQ-6 (§14) Hosting: GCP Cloud Run as specified (Terraform); Railway not implemented.
- OQ-7 (§14) Embeddings: Voyage `voyage-3` (1024-dim) default behind `EmbeddingProvider`; no in-region LLM endpoint for India (config flag reserved).
- OQ-8 (§13.4) The spec says the first loop iteration should stop after writing tasks.json for human review. The operator asked for autonomous completion, so the loop continues into M0 without waiting; tasks.json remains open for review and edits at any time.
- OQ-9 (§6 stage 3) The spec does not name a model class for the match rationale. Using Sonnet-class (`LLM_MODEL_RATIONALE`) as the cost-safe default; changeable in config.
- OQ-10 Local dev machine has no ClamAV or Tesseract binaries; both are behind pluggable interfaces with a no-op/fake in tests and real implementations in the Docker image.

- OQ-11 (§10.1 auth) Auth.js needs a database adapter for email magic links even with JWT sessions. Frontend ships a process-local in-memory adapter attached only when EMAIL_SERVER is set (marked TODO(M1)); the backend must expose users/accounts/verification_tokens so a real adapter replaces it. Also `resolveMembership(email)` in the frontend is an env-fallback stub until the backend membership lookup exists.
- OQ-12 (§10.1 files) MinIO no longer publishes public Docker images (docker.io and quay.io both deny anonymous pulls as of 2026-09-27). Local compose uses RustFS (S3/MinIO API-compatible, same ports and credential envs). Production stays GCS.
- OQ-13 (local dev) Docker Desktop's `credsStore: desktop` credential helper hangs on this machine (no interactive keychain), blocking every image pull. Workaround: run compose with `DOCKER_CONFIG` pointing at a copy of ~/.docker that omits `credsStore` (see docs/runbooks/local-docker.md once written). Images are now cached locally so plain `make up` works.
- OQ-14 (§2 rows 7-9, §14) Indian portal pre-flight: mahatenders.gov.in robots.txt is `Disallow: /` so Maharashtra must NOT be crawled (ship as link-only, health robots_disallowed). tender.telangana.gov.in and eproc.karnataka.gov.in are not GePNIC (custom platforms; Karnataka unreachable). Confirmed GePNIC portals with permissive robots: tntenders.gov.in (Tamil Nadu), etender.up.nic.in (Uttar Pradesh), etenders.gov.in (central). Proposed v1 GePNIC set: TN, UP, central GePNIC; Telangana via its own portal in phase 2. bidplus.gem.gov.in refused connections from this host; GeM adapter is fixture-driven until smoke-tested from a cloud region.

- OQ-15 (CLAUDE.md core purity vs M0 design) The tenant session factory lives at app/core/db.py as decided for M0, although CLAUDE.md says core/ is I/O-free. It is exercised by integration tests against the compose Postgres, so the core coverage gate still measures it honestly. If a later milestone prefers purity, move it to app/db.py and keep `get_session(tenant_id)` re-exported.

- OQ-16 (§3/§11 users table) Postgres applies the SELECT policy to INSERT ... RETURNING, so the app role (which only sees users through a membership in the current tenant) can never insert a brand-new user. Decision: users and their first membership are provisioned by the owner role inside app/services/users.py (JIT on first authenticated request, and by the seed); every other users read/write goes through the app role under RLS.

- OQ-17 (§11 auth rate limit) The per-IP failed-auth limiter is in-process (app/core/ratelimit.FixedWindowLimiter) for M0; with several Cloud Run instances the effective limit is N x 20/min. A Redis-backed limiter with the same hit/is_limited interface is a follow-up in the hardening milestone.
- OQ-18 (§3 roles) RBAC uses the role claim in the Auth.js token; the memberships row is created JIT and never re-roled by the API. Role changes therefore take effect at the next token refresh. If the DB must be authoritative per request, add a membership lookup in get_current_user (one indexed query).

- OQ-19 (§10.1 files, local dev) With STORAGE_BACKEND=local, GET /files/{id}/url returns an HMAC-signed `local://files/<key>?expires=&signature=` token that nothing serves yet (LocalStorage.verify() validates it). Local end-to-end downloads should use STORAGE_BACKEND=s3 against the compose RustFS (verified working, presigned GET 200); a dev-only download route can be added if the frontend needs the local backend. Also: SCANNER_BACKEND defaults to noop; deployed images must set clamav (uploads fail closed with 503 when clamd is unreachable). S3_BUCKET was split into S3_BUCKET_US/S3_BUCKET_IN (+ GCS_*) for residency.

- OQ-20 (§4.2 SBA table) app/core/data/sba_size_standards.csv is the SBA table effective 2023-03-17 (974 industries; the latest published on data.sba.gov at build time). Exception rows ("541330 (Exception 1)", dredging, IT value-added resellers footnote 18) and asset-based standards (commercial banking $850M assets) are not representable as one receipts/employee cap and resolve to `unknown`; the footnote column is carried for the 18 footnoted codes. SBA receipts are USD only, so an INR turnover average never feeds the receipts comparison (employee-based codes still resolve). Refresh the CSV when SBA publishes a new table (docs in app/core/data/README.md).

- OQ-21 (§10.3 profiles API) SPEC 10.3 lists only GET/PUT /profiles/{id}; the API also exposes GET /api/v1/profiles (list, tenant-scoped) because the wizard and plan checks need to find a tenant's profiles, and DELETE is intentionally absent (profiles are the anchor for matches/pursuits; deactivate via is_active=false). Sub-resources beyond the spec's list (vehicles, insurance, rate-card) follow the same CRUD shape. Profile `version` now bumps only when a value actually changes (identical re-PUTs are no-ops), which is what the match/rationale cache key wants.
- OQ-22 (§5.3 money) FX rates are a static JSON setting (FX_RATES, USD included so the table is the single source of truth; core.money.to_usd records the rate used per conversion). Production needs a rates job (RBI reference rate or a paid feed) that refreshes the table and stamps `rate_as_of`; until then estimated_value_usd on Indian records carries the deploy-time rate. Owner decision pending on the source.
- OQ-23 (§4.1/§6 India eligibility) Decisions in core.eligibility_in pending owner review: (a) MSE and Startup relaxations (turnover, experience, EMD waiver) apply only when the tender's extracted flags allow them (`allows_mse_exemption` / `allows_startup_exemption`), since GFR 2017 rule 170/173 relaxations are tender-specific; a profile that holds the status gets a 'relaxation not offered by this tender' note on a fail instead. (b) Udyam 'medium' is not an MSE. (c) EMD is informational (never fails, excluded from the SPEC 6 signal) because the profile has no bank-guarantee limit check yet; wire bonding_capacity later if wanted. (d) A DSC that is missing from the profile is `unknown` (missing data = 0.5), while an expired DSC or one expiring before `due_on`, and a missing/expired GeM seller ID, are blocking fails. (e) Experience years = today.year - year_founded unless the snapshot carries an explicit figure; past-performance durations are not used yet.
- OQ-24 (§7/§9 API date shape) SPEC 7 says API date fields include UTC and buyer_tz but not the wire shape. Decided: every date field is a `TzDateOut` object {utc (ISO 'Z'), buyer_tz, buyer_local (ISO with offset), buyer_display, user_tz?, user_local?, user_display?, display} built by core.display_time.tz_fields, with `user_tz` taken from user_notification_prefs.tz (M1-06) when the request is authenticated and omitted otherwise; email and UI render the same `display` string. Countdown floors to two units ('3d 4h', '6h 12m') and reports 'due now' within one minute either way. If the frontend prefers raw ISO plus client-side formatting, `buyer_local`/`user_local` already carry offsets.
- OQ-25 (§4/§10.4) Autofill needs a profile id (POST /profiles/{id}/autofill) but SPEC puts profile creation at the end of step 1: the wizard creates a draft profile (legal name + region) when autofill is first run on step 1, and step 1's save then PUTs; without autofill the profile is created on save as specified. Also: target_us_states/value_*_usd are shown only to US and target_in_states/value_*_inr only to IN although the API accepts both.

- OQ-30 (§5.3 set_aside "enum") SAM.gov publishes ~20 set-aside codes (SBA, 8A, HZC, SDVOSBC, WOSB, EDWOSB, ...) and Indian portals use free-text reservations. `set_aside` and `reservation` are stored as short text codes (normalised in app/core/normalize) rather than a Postgres enum, so a new code never breaks ingestion. Matching (M3) should compare codes, not display names.
- OQ-31 (§5.1 / M2-01) Where does the `sources` row sync run? On API startup (lifespan, best-effort: a DB outage logs a warning and the API still boots) and inside `run_source`'s caller (M2-16 job entrypoint should call `sync_sources` first). The FixtureAdapter lives in tests (tests/adapters/fixture_adapter.py) and is registered only for the duration of a test via `registry.temporarily()`, so no `fixture` source row appears in real databases.
- OQ-32 (M2-03 vs M2-01 order) `sources`/`source_runs` are created by migration 0003 already in M2-01 (acceptance needs them); 0003 currently revises 0001_m0_foundation and is re-pointed to 0002_m1_profile by the orchestrator at merge.
- OQ-33 (§5.2 SAM pagination) The Get Opportunities docs call `offset` a "page index" but every working client (and the acceptance line "offset until totalRecords") treats it as a record offset (offset += limit). The adapter uses record offsets; if a live smoke shows page-index semantics, change one line in `_fetch_window` (offset += 1) and the cursor stays valid.
- OQ-34 (§5.3 source time zone) SAM.gov returns responseDeadLine with an explicit UTC offset but postedDate/archiveDate as bare dates; naive values are interpreted in America/New_York and `source_tz` is recorded as America/New_York for every SAM notice (the buyer office's actual zone is not published). The UI shows the buyer zone alongside the user's, so a West-coast office deadline displays in ET, as SAM itself does.
- OQ-35 (M2-04 fixture provenance) There is no SAM.gov API key on this machine, so tests/adapters/fixtures/sam_opps/page{1,2}.json are SYNTHESIZED from the official Get Opportunities v2 documentation (field names, shapes and the documented sample record); values are invented. Grants.gov and USAspending fixtures are real captures. Replace the SAM fixture with a live capture during the nightly smoke (M2-14).
- OQ-36 (§5.2 SAM description quota) Pulling the description link costs one API call per notice, which a 10/day non-federal key cannot afford for a 1,000-record page. `fetch()` therefore yields listing records only; `fetch_detail(external_id)` (search by noticeid + description link) and header probing in `fetch_documents` are meant to run for NEW or CHANGED notices from the ingest pipeline / a detail job (M2-09/M2-16 wiring), and can be disabled with `probe_document_headers = False`. With a system-account key the job can simply call fetch_detail for every record.
- OQ-37 (§5.2 Grants.gov incremental) search2 has no "posted since" parameter; the UI's `dateRange` buckets (3, 7, 14, 21, 28, 35, 42, 49, 56 days; values taken from the live response's dateRangeOptions) are sent instead, picking the smallest bucket covering `since`. Older watermarks list everything posted+forecasted (a few thousand rows, no key needed). If a live smoke shows search2 ignoring dateRange, the adapter still works, just less incrementally.
- OQ-38 (M2-06 fixture provenance) tests/adapters/fixtures/grants_gov/search2_page{1,2}.json and fetch_332894.json are REAL captures (token redacted, publisher id and contact name anonymised, long arrays trimmed, one duplicate forecast hit 999001 added to page 2 to exercise keep-latest). fetch_forecast_334905.json is SYNTHESIZED (the capture only had a synopsis): its `forecast` block mirrors the synopsis field set plus the documented est* dates; replace with a live capture when the nightly smoke runs.
- OQ-39 (§5.2 USAspending semantics) USAspending awards are not opportunities, but every registered adapter must satisfy `normalize() -> OpportunityIn` (M2-14 contract tests). The adapter therefore emits award notices (notice_type award, status awarded) while the weekly job `services/spend.run_spend_stats` consumes the `AwardRecord` carried in raw.meta and never writes those awards to `opportunities`. Awards are bucketed by the fiscal year of their START date (fallback end date) and the query window is the last 3 fiscal years by action date; the API caps a query at 50,000 results (page 500 x 100), so profiles with very broad NAICS lists should pass `naics_codes` to narrow the query. Recompete candidates land in awards_enrichment with opportunity_id NULL, match_method recompete_candidate, recompete_watch true; M2-08 links them to notices.
- OQ-40 (§2 row 2 SAM.gov contract awards) No SAM key and no public capture of the contract-awards search exist on this machine, and the successor of the retired ATOM feed is documented only at the data-element level. The adapter therefore takes the endpoint from `SAM_AWARDS_API_URL` (default https://api.sam.gov/contract-awards/v1/search), the NAICS list from `SAM_AWARDS_NAICS` (or the constructor, so M1 profile codes can feed it), sends api_key + naics + signedDateFrom/To (MM/dd/yyyy, <= 1 year) + limit/offset, and reads responses through `core.normalize.sam_awards.ALIASES` (PIID, modificationNumber, solicitationNumber, effectiveDate, ultimateCompletionDate, obligatedAmount, baseAndAllOptionsValue, numberOfOffersReceived, naicsCode, productOrServiceCode, agency/department/office names, vendor.legalBusinessName/ueiSAM) with several accepted spellings each. tests/adapters/fixtures/sam_awards/page1.json is SYNTHETIC. First live smoke: confirm the endpoint and add any renamed keys to ALIASES / RESULT_KEYS (data changes only).
- OQ-41 (§5.4 versions on creation) A brand-new opportunity is stored at version 1 WITHOUT an opportunity_versions row; the versions table holds changes only (so "ingest twice with a moved deadline" yields exactly one version row). `opportunity.created` is published for new records and `opportunity.amended` for changes; matching (M3) should subscribe to both. The bus is in-process (services/events.py) with a `publisher` hook where the Celery producer plugs in at M2-16.
- OQ-42 (§5.4 detail freshness) `fetch_detail` (SAM description, header probes) is not called by the ingest pipeline itself; the runner ingests what `fetch()` returns. Wiring a detail pass for created/amended records (subscribe to the two events, call fetch_detail + fetch_documents, re-ingest) belongs with the scheduler task M2-16 / document parsing M2-12 so it can respect the SAM daily quota.

- OQ-43 (§5.4 dedupe scope) Both dedupe paths (cross-source key and fuzzy title) only merge records from DIFFERENT sources: within one source a repeated solicitation number is an amendment and is linked via parent_opportunity_id (M2-09), and same-source near-identical titles are usually re-postings the source itself distinguishes by external_id. `duplicate_of` is set once and never flipped when the loser later becomes richer (stable ids for matches/pursuits); the survivor's `extra.also_from` keeps every merged source link and is excluded from content_hash/diffs (VOLATILE_EXTRA_KEYS) so re-ingest of the survivor neither drops it nor records a version. The fuzzy window uses `response_due_at BETWEEN due - 1d AND due + 1d` (index-friendly, identical to abs(epoch diff) <= 86400). Search (M2-15) hides rows with duplicate_of set.

- OQ-44 (§5.4 derived status at ingest) `status` is derived from the deadline at ingest time too (a notice first seen 3 days before its deadline is created as closing_soon, one seen after it as closed) so the 15-minute job only has to move rows whose deadline crossed a boundary since the last run; a deadline moved back into the future re-opens a closed/closing_soon notice (change kinds deadline_moved + status_changed). Terminal statuses (cancelled/awarded) are source-stated and never touched by the clock; when a cancellation/award notice has a parent (same solicitation number) the parent takes the same status immediately at ingest and the job re-checks parents as a catch-up for out-of-order ingestion. Status-only versions reuse the row's content_hash (the source payload did not change).

- OQ-45 (§8 agent 1 / M2-12 parsed text format) Parsed text is stored as ONE text object per document (parsed/{opportunity_id}/{document_id}.txt) with pages joined by form feed (\f) so `text.split("\f")` restores page numbers for citations; sections (heading heuristic: numbered / ALL-CAPS / SECTION-PART-ANNEXURE keywords, DOCX heading styles, one per XLSX sheet) are recomputed on read and not stored. DOCX has no fixed pagination: pages follow explicit page breaks (else one page). Hindi in the text-PDF fixture is a transliteration marker because PyMuPDF's built-in fonts cannot draw Devanagari; the OCR path is covered with FakeOCR, real `eng+hin` Tesseract runs only in the Docker image (OQ-10, OCR_BACKEND=tesseract). Downloading documents is not part of ingest yet: `download_document` exists and the detail/document pass is wired by the scheduler (M2-16 / OQ-42).

- OQ-46 (§8 / M5-01 cost ledger units and model ids) usage_ledger.quantity is an int32, so LLM cost is recorded as `llm_cost_microusd` (integer micro-dollars; a monthly SUM is bigint) next to `llm_tokens_in` / `llm_tokens_out`, all three treated as MONTHLY periods in core.plan (MONTHLY_METRICS) so the M5 cost guard can budget per month; agent_steps.cost_usd keeps the exact Decimal. The default Haiku id is now `claude-haiku-4-5` (the API's exact id; the dated suffix was wrong) and LLM_PRICES defaults for the three classes live in config.py (input/output/cache_read/cache_write USD per Mtok) so cost_usd is computed, never guessed; an unpriced model raises. JSON outputs use a forced `emit` tool with strict input_schema (works on Opus/Sonnet/Haiku classes); if a model class that rejects forced tool_choice is ever configured, switch complete_json to output_config.format (structured outputs) in one place. The runtime lands as migration 0004_m5_agent_runtime (merge order, CLAUDE.md); later M5 tasks edit it in place.

- OQ-47 (§5.3 summary_ai billing and timing) Opportunities are global, so their enrichment cost cannot be charged to the tenant that happened to trigger the fetch: the summary runs through AgentRunner under the INTERNAL tenant (slug 'internal', looked up once through the owner role because RLS hides `tenants` from a no-tenant session) as run kind `summary_ai`, so agent_runs/agent_steps/usage_ledger rows exist for platform cost reporting; without an internal tenant (unseeded dev DB) or without an LLM (no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY) enrichment is skipped with a warning and ingestion is unaffected. The subscriber runs inside the ingest transaction via `Event.context["session"]` (the row is not committed when the event fires) so summary and notice commit together; a 1,000-record SAM run therefore makes up to 1,000 sequential Haiku calls inside the job. When M2-16 routes events through Celery the same `SummaryEnricher.summarize(session, opportunity_id)` can run from a worker task instead (the cache check by summary_version makes it idempotent).

- OQ-48 (§5.1 / §12 "malformed page") Two failure classes are kept apart. A 200 JSON page whose shape is wrong (records that are not objects, missing ids, non-integer counts, garbage field types) or whose keys were renamed (layout change) is absorbed: fetch() skips the bad records, yields nothing for a renamed results key, and health() turns DEGRADED with a message naming the problem (DegradedNotes in adapters/base.py; the nightly smoke and the sources health listing surface it). HTTP errors, retry exhaustion and non-JSON bodies (maintenance HTML) stay exceptions from fetch(): the runner records them as `failing`, keeps the watermark and cursor, and the M2-04..08 tests that assert those exceptions remain valid. Contract fixtures therefore are 200 JSON pages: <normal first page>, malformed.json, layout_change.json per adapter; Grants.gov is exercised in listing-only mode (with_details=False) so one route serves the page. `make smoke` exits 0 with {"status": "skipped"} unless BIDRADAR_LIVE=1; nightly-smoke.yml sets it, needs SAM_API_KEY / OPS_SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL secrets and SAM_AWARDS_NAICS / CONTACT_EMAIL variables, and treats "nothing checked" as a failure.

- OQ-49 (§10.3 / M2-15 search) Search hides rows with `duplicate_of` set (the survivor carries the merged links; `include_duplicates=true` shows them), orders by soonest deadline when there is no query and by ts_rank when there is; `q` is Postgres websearch syntax (quotes, -term, OR) over the same expression as ix_opportunities_fts (EXPLAIN with enable_seqscan=off proves the FTS and NAICS GIN indexes are usable). `min_score` is validated (0-100) and accepted but not applied and `match` is null until the matches table lands (M4); the p95 < 500 ms on 50k rows acceptance line is measured by the load script in M7-10, not here. Source attribution = {source_id, display name from core/attribution.py, the record's source_url or the portal home} plus the SPEC 11 disclaimer on every item. Opportunities are global, so the cross-tenant harness lists both routes with a `shared` (non-tenant) object and only checks that B's 200 carries none of A's ids.

- OQ-50 (§10.1 / M2-16 scheduling boundaries) Celery tasks are thin sync wrappers: each task runs `asyncio.run` over the async job with a FRESH Database (asyncpg pools are bound to the loop that created them) and disposes it afterwards; the worker also installs the summary_ai subscriber on its own EventBus so ingest inside a worker enriches like the API process does. The EventBus `publisher` hook is NOT wired to Celery yet: the in-process subscribers (summary_ai) already run where ingest runs, and fanning events out to a worker would double-run them until subscribers are made idempotent per event id (they are idempotent per opportunity version). The admin "run now" endpoint queues `bidradar.run_source` and falls back to running inline when no broker answers within CELERY_BROKER_CONNECT_TIMEOUT (or when the body says inline: true); `adapter.failing` is published by run_source itself after > 2 consecutive failing runs so CLI, Celery and admin runs all raise it; the ops-channel subscriber lands with notifications (M4). The SAM detail/document pass (fetch_detail + download_document + parse_and_store for created/amended notices, OQ-36/42) is still not scheduled; wire it as a second task consuming opportunity.created/amended once the event fan-out above exists, respecting the SAM daily quota.

- OQ-51 (§2 row 11 / §11 paid feeds) A paid-feed licence is a Secret Manager REFERENCE resolved at run time (`projects/.../versions/latest` via GcpSecretResolver, or an environment-variable name locally via EnvSecretResolver); the value lives only on the adapter instance and never in settings, health messages, repr or logs. A tenant-supplied licence makes the fetched records tenant-scoped, but `opportunities` is a global table: before any PaidFeedAdapter is enabled with a tenant key, the pipeline needs a tenant-scoped sink (or a tenant_id column on the notice) and the M3/M4 design must decide whether licensed records are shown only to the licensee. All six paid feeds and the four portal stubs ship `enabled = False`; the admin listing shows them as not_implemented and the run endpoint answers 409.
- OQ-52 (§4.3/§8 M1-12 re-index timing) Knowledge-base re-indexing is hooked into the four KB sub-resource routers (files, boilerplate, past-performance, service-lines), not into PUT /profiles/{id} (no company_profiles column is indexed). With CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER the profile is re-indexed INLINE inside the request transaction (chunks commit atomically with the mutation; the request pays for parsing + embedding), otherwise `bidradar.index_profile` is enqueued from a SQLAlchemy after_commit listener on the request session (FastAPI 0.141 runs BackgroundTasks before the yield-dependency commit, so a worker would have read stale rows). Only capability_statement/brochure/case_study/past_proposal files of kind pdf/docx/xlsx/text are indexed (brand assets, templates and images are skipped with a warning); an unparseable file keeps its previous chunks. Text sources hash their assembled text, file sources hash (sha256|kind|title) so an unchanged file is never downloaded again.
- OQ-53 (§5.3/§10.1 opportunity embeddings) opportunities.embedding is filled by an event subscriber (created/amended, installed after the summary enricher so the text includes summary_ai) from title + summary_ai + description_text[:3200] (else the first parsed document chunk); it is skipped, not failed, when the provider is unavailable (no VOYAGE_API_KEY) and Voyage cost for global notices is not metered in usage_ledger (no embedding metric yet; add one with the M5 cost guard if needed). document_chunks.embedding stays NULL until M4/M5 decide whether whole solicitation documents are embedded. Tests run with EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=fake (FakeEmbeddings: sha1-bucketed bag-of-words, unit-normalised) and CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER=true via the shared `settings` fixture.
- OQ-54 (§4/§10.3 M1-10 autofill contract) Suggestion `field` follows the frontend's dotted grammar (frontend/src/lib/autofill.ts): scalar profile columns (`phone`, not `main_phone`), `dba_names[]`/`addresses[]` appends, `codes.<scheme>[]` with value {code, is_primary}, and list resources whose value IS the child POST body (service_lines[], certifications[], past_performance[], personnel[]). The company overview is emitted as `boilerplate[]` = BoilerplateIn body (kind company_overview, HTML body); the frontend's LIST_RESOURCES table has no `boilerplate` entry yet, so that suggestion is displayed but cannot be applied until the frontend adds `boilerplate: "boilerplate"` (frontend agent). Suggestions for the other region's fields/schemes are dropped server-side (NAICS/PSC are US schemes, GeM/India categories IN) with one warning; an IN profile sending `uei` gets the usual 422 region_mismatch. The route never writes the profile: the only rows it creates are agent_runs/agent_steps/usage_ledger (run kind profile_autofill, metered under the caller's tenant) and the audit row profile.autofill.
- OQ-55 (§4.1/§5.1 SAM Entity API and LLM schema) tests/adapters/fixtures/sam_entity/entity_ALPHA1234567.json is SYNTHESIZED from the public Entity Management API v3 docs (no key on this machine; README in the folder); the mapping covers entityRegistration, coreData.entityInformation/physicalAddress/generalInformation/businessTypes (SBA-certified list at 0.95, self-asserted business types at 0.8) and assertions.goodsAndServices; the entity call is made through PoliteClient with respect_robots=False (JSON API) and archive=False (the api_key travels as a query parameter). Without SAM_API_KEY the UEI path answers 200 with a warning and no network call (isolation factory relies on this). The website fetch is a 2 MB-capped PoliteClient GET (robots.txt honoured: a Disallow is a warning, never bypassed) run in a worker thread; non-HTML content types and HTTP errors are warnings. AutofillExtraction has optional fields with defaults, so its JSON schema lists no `required` keys: if the Anthropic strict tool mode rejects that for a real model, switch emit_tool to non-strict for this schema or make every field required-with-null (same as the OQ-46 note for structured outputs).

- OQ-56 (§10.1 / M7-04 billing provider choice) The provider follows `tenants.region` (us -> Stripe/USD, in -> Razorpay/INR), not `data_residency` and not a per-tenant setting: a tenant that sells from India but stores in the US is not modelled. Plan ids are config maps (`STRIPE_PRICE_IDS` / `RAZORPAY_PLAN_IDS`, {plan: provider id}) so no price lives in code; a plan with no configured id makes checkout 502 rather than guessing. Both providers are called over raw REST with httpx (no SDK) so tests can mock every request with respx and no vendor client ends up in the dependency tree; the cost is that we own the request shapes (Stripe form-encoded, Razorpay JSON) and must follow their API versions by hand. `POST /billing/checkout` never changes the plan; only a verified webhook does, so a user who abandons the hosted page keeps the old plan.
- OQ-57 (§10.3 / M7-04 webhook trust and idempotency) Webhooks are the only unauthenticated mutating routes (PUBLIC_ROUTES in tests/isolation/factories.py): authentication is the provider HMAC over the RAW body (Stripe `t=<ts>,v1=<hex>` over "<ts>.<payload>" with a 300 s replay window; Razorpay hex HMAC of the body) and nothing is read from the payload before it verifies — a bad, missing, stale or tampered signature is 400 with no row written. Idempotency is a unique (provider, event_id); Razorpay does not put an id in the body, so `X-Razorpay-Event-Id` is used and, when absent, "<event>:<entity id>" — a provider that redelivers with a fresh header id would double-count, which is why plan changes are idempotent by value (set tenants.plan, no deltas). The tenant is resolved from metadata/notes first (Stripe `metadata.tenant_id` + `client_reference_id`, Razorpay `notes.tenant_id`) and only then from billing_customers on the owner role; an event that resolves to no tenant is answered 200 "ignored" so the provider stops retrying something we can never attribute.
- OQ-58 (§11 / M7-04 audit of webhooks) A webhook has no user, so the audit middleware (which needs `request.state.user` for a tenant) writes nothing for it. `BillingService.apply_event` therefore writes its own audit_log row (action `billing.webhook`, user_id NULL, object tenant, meta provider/kind/event_id/plan_before/plan_after) inside the same transaction as the plan change, and `test_every_mutating_route_under_api_v1_is_audited` now exempts exactly the two webhook routes with the exempt set asserted so no other route can slip out. An unsigned webhook (400) leaves no audit row at all: there is no verified tenant to write it into, and audit_log.tenant_id is NOT NULL.
- OQ-59 (§10.1 / M7-04 GST invoices) Invoices are issued by Razorpay; we record the GST split, we do not generate the PDF. `core.billing.gst_breakdown` treats the charged amount as GST-INCLUSIVE, rounds the taxable value and derives tax as (total - taxable) so the lines always add up to what was charged; intra-state (place of supply == the first two digits of BILLING_GSTIN) splits CGST/SGST, otherwise IGST. Defaults: SAC 998314, 18% (`BILLING_GST_RATE_PCT`). Place of supply comes from the customer's `notes.place_of_supply`, else the state code of their GSTIN — a customer with neither is billed as inter-state (IGST), which is the safe default but should be confirmed with a CA before the first Indian invoice, together with reverse charge for unregistered recipients and the export-of-services case (§14 counsel item).

- OQ-60 (§11 / M7-07 consent at signup) There is no auth/signup route on main yet (`/api/v1/auth/*` is reserved in PUBLIC_ROUTES but unimplemented), so "consent notice recorded at signup" is served by `POST /api/v1/me/consents` {kind, version}: the signup wizard calls it right after the first `/me` provisioning call, and Settings > Privacy calls it again when a version is bumped. The route is open to EVERY tenant role (a viewer must be able to consent for themselves) and is idempotent per (tenant, user, kind, version) — re-accepting returns the original row with created=false, while bumping `DPDP_NOTICE_VERSION` / `PRIVACY_POLICY_VERSION` / `TERMS_VERSION` writes a new row and never touches the old one. Nothing yet BLOCKS an un-consented user from using the API; enforcing that belongs with the auth task that owns signup.
- OQ-61 (§11 / M7-07 what an erasure removes) `delete_tenant` derives the table list from SQLAlchemy metadata (every table with a tenant_id column) and deletes children-first in reverse `sorted_tables` order, so a table added later is erased the day it exists; the integration test re-derives the same list from information_schema, which would also catch a table the ORM does not know about. audit_log is the single retained table (SPEC 11: the trail must outlive the data) and the `tenants` row is kept and stamped `deleted_at` so the id can never be reused; `POST /tenant/{export,delete}` answer 409 afterwards. Global tables are deliberately untouched: `opportunities` and friends are public notices owned by nobody. `users` rows are global too, so an erasure deletes only those left with no membership anywhere — a user who also belongs to another tenant keeps their account. The data_requests row that asked for the deletion is itself deleted (it is tenant-scoped), so the outcome lives in the retained `privacy.tenant_delete` audit row, written before the deletes so it shares their transaction.
- OQ-62 (§11 / M7-07 export shape and delivery) A tenant export is one zip written to the tenant's own residency bucket at `tenants/{tenant_id}/exports/{request_id}.zip`: `manifest.json` (row counts, file count, region, generated_at, files_missing_from_storage), `tables/<name>.json` per tenant-scoped table and `files/<file id>-<name>` for every uploaded object. It is registered as a normal `files` row so the owner downloads it through the existing signed-URL route, and the request also carries a ready signed URL in `details.url`. Two consequences to revisit at scale: the whole export is built in memory (fine for an MVP tenant, not for a tenant with gigabytes of documents — stream to storage when that happens) and the export zip is itself a tenant file, so it is included in the NEXT export and removed by an erasure. No retention/expiry job deletes old exports yet.
- OQ-63 (§11 / M7-07 who answers a correction or erasure of one principal) `access` is answered inline (the response carries the caller's own rows from memberships, consents, data_requests, user_notification_prefs, files they uploaded and their audit_log entries) because we can compute it exactly. `correction` and `erasure` for a SINGLE data principal inside a live tenant stay `received` on the SLA clock for a human: deleting one member's rows from a shared tenant (their drafts, their uploads, their audit trail) is a judgement call the spec does not settle, and the tenant's own retention obligations may conflict. `sla_due_at` is created_at + `DATA_REQUEST_SLA_DAYS` (30) and `overdue` is computed on read, but nothing alerts on it yet — wire the SLA breach into the ops channel with the notification work.

- OQ-64 (§10.1 / M7-05 how traces reach Cloud Trace) The application speaks plain OTLP/HTTP to `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` (`<endpoint>/v1/traces`) and knows nothing about GCP: the Cloud Trace credentials belong to an OpenTelemetry Collector sidecar on the Cloud Run service, so the same image runs unchanged in the US and Indian deployments and in local dev against `otel/opentelemetry-collector`. No endpoint means no TracerProvider is installed at all (the global stays the API's no-op default) and no exporter thread starts; `google-cloud-trace` is deliberately NOT a dependency. Instrumented: FastAPI (excluding /healthz), SQLAlchemy, httpx (so every adapter fetch is a child span) and — worker only — Celery. Adapter runs are therefore traced as the Celery task span plus its httpx children rather than a hand-rolled span per adapter; add an explicit span in `services.source_runner` if per-source timing needs to be a first-class attribute rather than a task attribute.
- OQ-65 (§11 / M7-05 what Sentry is allowed to see) `send_default_pii=False`, `max_request_body_size="never"` and a `before_send` that (a) redacts any field named like a secret or a regulated identifier at any depth (authorization, cookie, stripe-signature, x-razorpay-signature, api_key, token, password, ein, pan, gstin, tan, bank_account, ifsc, email) and (b) masks email / EIN / PAN / GSTIN / "Bearer <token>" shapes INSIDE free text, because the leak we actually fear is a solicitation excerpt or a stack-frame local, not a tidy field. The value patterns are deliberately conservative (an over-eager regex makes crash reports useless), so an identifier in an unusual format can still slip through — the real defence is that bodies are never sent. The event is tagged tenant_id / request_id and the user is attached as an ID ONLY, never an email. `SENTRY_TRACES_SAMPLE_RATE` defaults to 0.0: OTel owns tracing, Sentry owns errors.
- OQ-66 (§10.1 / M7-05 request context in logs) `app/core/context.py` gained tenant_id/user_id contextvars next to the request id; `get_current_user` binds them the moment the bearer token verifies and `RequestIdMiddleware` clears both in its finally block, so `app/logging.py` can stamp request_id/tenant_id/user_id on EVERY structlog line without any call site passing them. Consequences worth knowing: a line emitted before authentication (public route, 401, middleware) carries only request_id — by design, and asserted; and a Celery task has no request, so its lines carry none of the three and the job entrypoints log tenant_id/source_id/request_id explicitly instead. JSON rendering is still tied to `APP_ENV=production`; there is no separate LOG_JSON switch.
- OQ-67 (§8 / M7-05 Langfuse tags) The Tracer protocol gained `run_started(run_id, kind, tenant_id, pursuit_id)`, called once by `AgentRunner.run`, so the Langfuse trace is created with tags `tenant:<uuid>`, `kind:<run kind>` and (when set) `pursuit:<uuid>` instead of being an untagged bag of generations. Per-step cost still goes to Langfuse as generation usage {input, output, total_cost}. Note that tagging with the raw tenant UUID sends a tenant identifier to Langfuse (EU) — acceptable because it is an opaque id and Langfuse is already a listed sub-processor for prompts and completions, but it is the reason the LLM trace destination must stay on the sub-processor list. Langfuse remains best-effort: the authoritative cost numbers are agent_steps.cost_usd and the usage_ledger llm_* metrics (OQ-46).
- OQ-68 (§11 / M7-01 ClamAV in the image) Virus definitions are NOT baked into the backend image: `freshclam` is ~300 MB and stale the day the image is built, so `backend/docker/clamd-start.sh` fetches them into /var/lib/clamav on first container start and then execs clamd. Consequences: the first minute of a cold container cannot scan, and `app/services/scanner.ClamAVScanner` already fails CLOSED (503, ScannerUnavailableError) while clamd is unreachable — which is the behaviour we want, but it means a cold-started api revision briefly rejects uploads. clamd is started only when SCANNER_BACKEND=clamav, so local/test containers never pay for it. If the cold-start window becomes a problem the fix is a sidecar with a persistent volume (or Cloud Run's mountable GCS bucket for the CVD files), not baking the DB into the image.
- OQ-69 (§10.1 / M7-01 Celery on Cloud Run) A Cloud Run *service* revision is only marked ready when the container answers on $PORT, but a Celery worker and beat speak only to Redis. `app/jobs/health_server.py` therefore starts a stdlib one-route HTTP server (`/healthz`, same JSON shape as the API plus a `component` field) next to Celery in the worker and beat containers, and the manifests set `run.googleapis.com/cpu-throttling: "false"` (a throttled worker stalls between requests) with minScale 1. Beat is pinned min=max=1 because two beats double every periodic task. The health route is deliberately shallow: it reports the process, not Postgres, so a wedged broker does not cause Cloud Run to recycle a container that is merely idle. If Cloud Run worker pools (no HTTP contract) become GA in these regions, worker/beat should move to them and this shim can go.
- OQ-70 (§10.1/§12 / M7-01 who owns the Cloud Run objects) `infra/cloudrun/**` is GENERATED by `python -m app.jobs.generate_cloudrun` from the adapter registry and committed so the Cloud Run surface shows up in review; `tests/unit/test_cloudrun_manifests.py` regenerates it and fails on drift, so a new adapter cannot land without its job. But Terraform (M7-02) is the authoritative deployer — the YAML is a readable spec plus an escape hatch for `gcloud run services replace` via `infra/cloudrun/render.sh`, and every environment-specific value is a `${PLACEHOLDER}` listed in `generate_cloudrun.PLACEHOLDERS`. Two consequences: the same facts (image, service account, connector) are expressed twice, so the two must be kept honest by review; and disabled adapters (`enabled = False`) get no job and no Scheduler trigger, which is asserted. Image sizes on this machine: backend 897 MB (Tesseract eng+hin + ClamAV dominate), frontend 321 MB; a clean backend build takes about 3 minutes.

- OQ-71 (§3/§11 / M7-08 what a support-access grant is) SPEC 3 says a platform admin has no tenant-data access "unless support access is granted and logged". The logged half already existed (audit_log via `support_access_session`); M7-08 adds the granted half as `support_access_grants`, a GLOBAL table (no tenant_id — it carries `target_tenant_id`, no RLS policy, no app-role DML grant at all, listed in RLS_EXEMPT_TABLES with that reasoning) written and read only through the owner-role admin session. A grant belongs to ONE admin and ONE tenant, is time-boxed (5..480 minutes, default 60, clamped in `core.admin.clamp_support_minutes`) and expires by itself; another admin's grant does not help. `require_support_access(tenant_id)` gates the only admin route that reads tenant-scoped rows today, GET /admin/tenants/{id}/audit-log, and answers 403 with an explanation otherwise. Two gaps on purpose: there is no revoke ENDPOINT yet (the `revoked_at` column exists and is honoured, so revoking is an owner-role UPDATE until the console needs a button), and no other admin route reads tenant content, so the dependency currently guards one route — every future one (drafts, exports during support) must take it too.
- OQ-72 (§10.3 / M7-08 admin route shapes and double auditing) GET /api/v1/admin/tenants changed from a bare list to a page object {items,total,page,page_size,pages} because the console needs q + pagination; the one test that read the list shape was updated. Mutating admin routes must NOT use `AdminSessionDep`: that dependency writes an `admin_access` audit row per use, and the audit middleware already writes exactly one row per mutation (asserted by test_every_mutating_route_under_api_v1_is_audited), so PATCH /admin/tenants/{id} takes a new `AdminWriteSessionDep` (owner session, no audit row of its own). A plan/is_internal change additionally writes ONE `admin.tenant.update` row into the TARGET tenant's trail — but only when a value actually changes, so an empty PATCH stays a single-row no-op and the tenant's own audit log is not polluted by admin reads that changed nothing. Same split as support access: the middleware row lands in the admin's (internal) tenant, the service row in the tenant being administered.
- OQ-73 (§10.4 screen 9 / M7-08 usage, cost and health) Usage is reported per tenant per 'YYYY-MM' from usage_ledger (llm_tokens_in/out, llm_cost_microusd, OQ-46) plus a count of agent_runs whose created_at falls in the month; `notifications` is in the response as an explicit null because SPEC 10.4 asks for it and the notifications table only arrives with M4 — fill it there rather than adding a second usage route. Cost crosses the wire as BOTH `cost_microusd` (integer, authoritative) and `cost_usd` (float, convenience); the frontend formats from the integer. GET /admin/health is deliberately cheap and synchronous: a `SELECT 1`, a kombu `ensure_connection` ping at CELERY_BROKER_CONNECT_TIMEOUT (a blocking call inside the event loop — acceptable for an admin-only route with a 2 s default, but it must not be added to /healthz), and a CONFIG-level storage check (backend + bucket names, `degraded` for the local filesystem backend) with no round trip to S3/GCS, so a slow bucket cannot hang the console. Adapter health is rolled up worst-first (failing > degraded > not_implemented > unknown > ok).
- OQ-74 (§11/§12 / M7-02 a deployment serves ONE residency) `app/services/storage.bucket_for_region` picks the bucket from the TENANT's `data_residency`, not from the deployment's REGION, so a `prod-us` deployment that somehow acquired an Indian tenant would need a GCS_BUCKET_IN. Rather than give it one, each environment sets the foreign region's bucket name to `<prefix>-no-foreign-residency`, a bucket that does not exist: the write fails loudly instead of silently landing Indian documents in Virginia. Consequence: nothing in the code prevents CREATING a foreign-residency tenant in the wrong deployment — it just cannot store files, so the failure surfaces as a 500 on the first upload. Signup should refuse the mismatch outright (the auth task owns signup, OQ-60). `terraform output residency_ok` is the standing check that bucket, CMEK key, DB, backups, Redis and the Secret Manager replicas are all in the environment's own region.
- OQ-75 (§11 / M7-02 who puts the secret values in) Terraform creates one EMPTY Secret Manager secret per `SECRET_SETTINGS` name and grants the three runtime service accounts `secretAccessor`; it writes a version for exactly three — DATABASE_URL, DATABASE_URL_OWNER and REDIS_URL — because it generated those passwords itself (`random_password`, so they DO land in Terraform state, which is why the state bucket is per-environment, in-region, and must be access-restricted like any other store). Every other value (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, SAM_API_KEY, Stripe, Razorpay, AUTH_SECRET, FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY, Sentry, Langfuse) is added by a human with `gcloud secrets versions add`. A Cloud Run revision therefore FAILS TO START until they exist — the right failure, but it means a fresh environment needs the secret-filling step in its runbook before the first deploy, and rotation is manual: a new version is picked up on the next revision, not live.
- OQ-76 (§13.1 / M7-02 Terraform state and preview revisions) The GCS backend is a PARTIAL configuration (`backend "gcs" {}` plus a committed `backend.hcl`) because a backend block cannot read variables; the `state_bucket` variable exists only so the intended bucket is in code and in an output, and a test asserts the two agree. Two further choices: `google_cloud_run_v2_service` sets `ignore_changes = [client, client_version, template[0].revision]` so the per-PR preview revisions M7-03 adds out of band survive the next apply — the cost is that a revision changed by hand is invisible to Terraform; and `google_kms_crypto_key` carries `prevent_destroy`, so tearing down even a dev environment needs a deliberate state removal, which is the trade we want when the alternative is permanently unreadable objects. `terraform apply` was NOT run (no GCP project exists yet): `fmt -check -recursive` and `validate` on all four envs passed locally against google provider 6.50.0, and both are now a CI job.
- OQ-77 (§12 / M7-03 what "same image" is enforced by) A tag promotion never rebuilds: `resolve` looks up the digest of `<repo>/backend:sha-<commit>` with `gcloud artifacts docker images describe` and every downstream job passes `…@sha256:…`, while the composite action REFUSES an image reference without `@sha256:` before it touches anything. So prod-us and prod-in run bytes identical to what staging-in ran. Two consequences: tagging a commit that main never built fails `resolve` with an explicit message rather than building something new; and the front-end image bakes `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL` at BUILD time, so the one image that reaches all four environments carries the DEV api URL in its bundle. That is wrong for production and is the one place the "same image" rule leaks — the frontend must either read its API base at runtime (move it out of NEXT_PUBLIC_) or be built per environment. Recorded rather than fixed here because it belongs with the frontend config task.
- OQ-78 (§12/§13.1 / M7-03 what a preview environment is, and is not) A preview is a TAGGED, ZERO-TRAFFIC revision of the dev api and web services (`gcloud run deploy --tag pr-<n> --no-traffic`), reachable at `https://pr-<n>---bidradar-dev-api-<hash>.a.run.app`, torn down by `--remove-tags pr-<n>` when the PR closes. It deliberately SHARES the dev database and runs NO migration: a per-PR Cloud SQL instance costs more than the review it enables, and the deploy pipeline already requires migrations to be backward compatible (schema first, traffic second). The consequence is real and worth knowing: a PR whose migration is not backward compatible will show a broken preview, because the new code is running against the old dev schema — that is information, not a bug in the preview. Previews are also skipped for forks, because a fork cannot mint an OIDC token for our project and giving it one would hand a stranger the deploy identity.
- OQ-79 (§13.1 / M7-03 approvals, rollback and what the pipeline will not do) staging-in, prod-us and prod-in are separate GitHub Environments, so "required reviewers" is configured in repository settings, not in YAML — the workflow only names the environment, and prod-us/prod-in are a matrix with `max-parallel: 1` so each gets its own approval and India does not go out while the US deploy is still failing. Rollback is a traffic shift (`update-traffic --to-revisions`), which does NOT roll back the database; a bad migration is recovered with PITR (docs/runbooks/restore-drill.md), and the runbook says so. The pipeline deliberately does NOT run `terraform apply`: a plan that can destroy a Cloud SQL instance is applied by a person after review. `workflow_dispatch` was also removed — a deploy is caused by a merge or a tag, and anything else is the by-hand gcloud sequence in the runbook.
- OQ-80 (§11 / M7-06 what happens when Redis is down) The API limiter FAILS OPEN: a Redis error is caught, logged once a minute, and the request is answered by an in-process bucket instead. A rate limiter protects availability and is not an authorisation control, so failing closed would convert a Redis blip into a total outage — the opposite trade from the ClamAV scanner, which fails CLOSED because it protects integrity (OQ-68). The consequence is that during a Redis outage the effective limit is N x the configured limit across N Cloud Run instances, which still blunts a stampede but does not enforce a per-tenant quota. Also deliberate: the token bucket is a bucket and not a fixed window, because a fixed window lets a client spend a full allowance at 59 s and again at 61 s; and a rejected request costs no tokens, so a hammering client recovers on schedule instead of being held down forever.
- OQ-81 (§11 / M7-06 how the limiter knows the tenant, and what it skips) The middleware runs BEFORE the routers, so it cannot use `get_current_user`; it verifies the bearer token itself with the same `decode_token` and treats any failure as "no tenant", falling back to the IP bucket while the 401 is produced downstream as usual. That means the JWT is verified twice per authenticated request (HS256 over a small payload, so cheap, but it is a real duplication). Two exemptions ship by default in `RATE_LIMIT_EXEMPT_PATHS`: `/healthz`, because a refused probe makes Cloud Run recycle a container, and `/api/v1/webhooks`, because Stripe and Razorpay are bursty and already authenticated by signature with a replay window — a webhook flood is therefore bounded by signature verification, not by the limiter. Default limits are 600/min per tenant and 120/min per IP, both settings; the M0 failed-auth limiter is unchanged and still per-instance (OQ-17).
- OQ-82 (§11 / M7-06 what the ASVS L2 checklist actually says) `docs/security/asvs-l2.md` covers all fourteen chapters with a four-value status, and `tests/unit/test_security_hardening.py` enforces that the vocabulary is closed, that not everything is green, and that every repository path AND symbol the evidence column cites still exists — evidence pointing at a renamed file reads as a control that exists. Seven controls are honestly open, in priority order: no security headers or CSP anywhere (V14.4, the biggest gap); no private-address filtering on tenant-supplied URLs, so application-layer SSRF is not ruled out (V12.6); no written threat model (V1.14); JWTs cannot be revoked before `exp` (V3.8); `FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY` rotation needs a re-encryption pass (V6.4.2); `/docs` is served in production (V14.3); and the prompt-injection eval golden set is still a placeholder (V5). SECURITY.md publishes the same list.
- OQ-83 (§11 / M7-06 the restore drill has been written, not run) `scripts/restore_drill.sh` lists automated backups, refuses one older than 36 hours, asserts PITR is on with >= 7 days of logs and that the backup location is in-region, creates a NEW scratch instance (refusing to proceed if the name exists, because `gcloud sql backups restore` into an existing instance overwrites it), restores, verifies through the Cloud SQL Auth Proxy that `alembic_version` is non-empty and that tenants/users/opportunities/audit_log are not all zero, then deletes the scratch instance even on failure. It has only been exercised with `--dry-run`: no GCP project exists yet, so the drill log in docs/runbooks/restore-drill.md is empty and SPEC 11's "restore drill before launch" is NOT yet satisfied — the tooling is, the drill is not. Two things it deliberately does not cover: GCS object restore (versioning exists; a bucket drill does not) and the fact that a database restore and an object-store restore can land at different points in time.

- OQ-60 (§2 row 7 / M3-02 CPPP secondary page) The design named `eprocure.gov.in/cppp/tendersbyorganisationnew` as the second captcha-free page, but the capture of that URL is the portal's 404 page ("Oops! Page not found"); only `latestactivetendersnew` (7-column table: Sl.No, e-Published, Closing, Opening, Title/Ref.No./Tender Id, Organisation Name, Corrigendum) was verified. The adapter therefore treats "Tenders by Organisation" as best-effort: `CPPP_BY_ORG_URL` (default `/cppp/tendersbyorganisation`, empty = off) is fetched once per run; a 7-column tender table is parsed directly, an organisation index (GePNIC shape: S.No / Organisation Name / Tender Count with a link per organisation) is followed for the first `CPPP_MAX_ORGS_PER_RUN` (25) organisations, and a 404 or unknown layout only turns the run DEGRADED while the primary page still flows. `byorg.html` and `org_listing.html` fixtures are SYNTHESIZED from the GePNIC organisation index and the real 7-column rows (with an "Organisation Chain" column); replace them with live captures at the first smoke from an Indian IP. The detail `tendersfullview/<token>` link is kept as `source_url` (token = 'A13h1'-separated base64 segments: internal id, hashes, unix expiry, reference, tender code; the internal id is the stable `external_id` and the expiry is stored in `extra.detail_link_expires_at`), every record is `detail_status = manual` with `extra.manual_detail_url = /cppp/tendersearch` (CAPTCHA), and `fetch_detail` makes no request at all.

- OQ-61 (§2 row 8 / M3-03 GeM shapes) bidplus.gem.gov.in refused connections from the build host (OQ-14), so the GeM adapter is fixture-driven end to end: `GEM_BIDS_URL` (default the JSON backend of the public All Bids page, `/all-bids-data`) is POSTed the form field `payload` = {"page", "param": {"searchBid", "searchType": "fullText"}, "filter": {"bidStatusType": "ongoing_bids", "byType": "all", "sort": "Bid-End-Date-Latest", ...}} and the answer is read as Solr-style `response.response.{numFound,start,docs[]}` with one-item list fields through `core.normalize.gem.ALIASES` (b_id, b_bid_number, b_category_name, b_total_quantity, ba_official_details_minName/deptName/orgName/officeName, final_start_date_sort / final_end_date_sort, b_bid_type, b_is_ra ...). The listing fixtures (all_bids_page1/2, malformed, layout_change) and the bid PDF (built with PyMuPDF by tests/adapters/fixtures/gem/make_fixtures.py in the layout of a real GeM bid document, with a prompt-injection decoy line in the ATC) are SYNTHESIZED; the first smoke from an Indian/cloud IP confirms the endpoint, a possible CSRF token requirement and the key names (data changes in ALIASES / the payload builder). The acceptance line "GeM seller registration link surfaced in source_url" is read as: `source_url` is the official per-bid page (`GEM_BID_PAGE_URL`, the showbidDocument link, so link-back stays a government page) and the registration link (`GEM_SELLER_REGISTRATION_URL`) travels in `extra.gem_seller_registration_url` for the UI; the exact registration URL is configuration.

- OQ-62 (§2 row 9 / M3-05 GePNIC states) The acceptance line names `gepnic_ts | gepnic_ka | gepnic_mh`, but the OQ-14 pre-flight contradicts all three: mahatenders.gov.in serves `robots.txt: Disallow: /` (never crawlable), eprocurement.telangana.gov.in is a *custom* platform (JS login front page, no captcha-free listing table) and not GePNIC at all, and no Karnataka GePNIC host answered from the build host. The generic class is therefore configured for the three portals whose robots.txt permits crawling and whose pages were captured — `gepnic_tn` (tntenders.gov.in), `gepnic_up` (etender.up.nic.in), `gepnic_central` (etenders.gov.in, path `/eprocure/app`) — while `gepnic_mh` (health `robots_disallowed`) and `gepnic_ts` (health `not_implemented`) stay in `gepnic_configs.yaml` with `enabled: false` so the source ids, attribution and admin health exist without a request ever being made; Karnataka is a one-row YAML addition once a host is confirmed. Second decision inside the same task: GePNIC's "Latest Active Tenders" page is CAPTCHA-gated (unlike CPPP's `latestactivetendersnew`), so it is documented in the config and **never fetched**; the two captcha-free pages are the front-page "Latest Tenders" marquee and "Tenders by Organisation" (index + the first `GEPNIC_MAX_ORGS_PER_RUN` organisation listings). The organisation index / listing fixtures are SYNTHESIZED from the GePNIC table structure (the mahatenders capture was read as a structure reference only and never fetched by the adapter); replace them at the first smoke from an Indian IP.

- OQ-63 (§12 / M3-07 Devanagari PDF fixture) No Indian portal PDF could be captured from the build host (OQ-14), and drawing Devanagari with PyMuPDF needs a Devanagari font — embedding a system font (Apple's DevanagariMT) in a committed fixture is a licensing question we do not need to answer. `backend/tests/adapters/fixtures/documents/make_hindi_fixture.py` therefore writes `hindi_tender.pdf` by hand: a Type0 / Identity-H font with one CID per Devanagari grapheme cluster (base letter + its matras, consonants joined by a virama — the same granularity a real Indic PDF uses) and a `ToUnicode` CMap mapping each CID back to its Unicode cluster, with no font program embedded. A viewer substitutes a font for the glyphs; the *text layer*, which is all that parsing/hashing/chunking/OCR-free extraction touches, is real Devanagari and comes back unbroken (a one-byte-per-character encoding was tried first and MuPDF split words after every anusvara). Replace it with a real Hindi tender PDF at the first live capture.

- OQ-64 (§11 / M3-10 exports and emails) The acceptance line "exports and emails carry the same attribution and the verify-on-portal disclaimer" has nothing to attach to yet: there is no export endpoint and `app/notify/` is empty (digests are M4, exports M7). The deliverable in this milestone is therefore the single place that composes the line — `core.disclaimers.attribution_text()` / `record_footer()` (pure, built on `core.attribution`) — rendered by the opportunities serializer as `attribution.text` / `attribution.footer`, plus `docs/legal.md` §2 stating that exports and notification emails MUST render `record_footer` instead of composing their own wording. Re-check this acceptance box when the export and digest tasks land.
- OQ-70 (§6 stage 1 "response date in the future") A notice whose status is `cancelled` or `awarded` is dropped by the same filter (reasons `status_cancelled` / `status_awarded`) even when its deadline is still ahead: scoring a cancelled tender would raise alerts for something nobody can bid on. Recompete watch (awards_enrichment.recompete_watch on the notice or its solicitation number) keeps past-due AND terminal notices, as the spec's parenthesis intends. A deadline that is unknown (None: forecasts, sources sought) is not "past" and is kept.
- OQ-71 (§6 set-aside cap) US set-aside codes are the SAM.gov codes stored by M2 (OQ-30): 8A/8AN -> 8a, HZC/HZS -> hubzone, WOSB(SS) -> wosb (edwosb also qualifies), EDWOSB(SS) -> edwosb, SDVOSB/SDVOSBC/SDVOSBS -> sdvosb, VSA/VSS/VOSB -> vosb (sdvosb also qualifies), SBA/SBP -> SBA small status for ANY of the notice's NAICS (core.eligibility). Programmes the profile cannot express (LAS local area, IEE/ISBEE Indian economic enterprise, BICiv) are recorded as "not evaluated" and never capped. An `unknown` size (no USD receipts / head count, no NAICS, no bundled standard) never caps either: the eligibility signal reports it as 0.5 rather than the hard filter penalising missing data. Indian reservations are still free text until the M3 adapters land, so `reservation` is classified by regex (MSE/MSME, SC/ST, women, startup, Class-I/Make in India) against Udyam category, mse_ownership, DPIIT number and local_supplier_class; when M3 normalises reservation codes, swap the regex table for a code map in one place (`core/matching/filters._IN_RULES`).
- OQ-72 (§6 blocked buyers) Blocked buyers match after core.normalize.buyer normalisation (case, punctuation, legal suffixes, leading "the") as an equal name OR a whole-word phrase inside any buyer level (org, sub-org, office, hierarchy), so blocking "Energy" blocks "Department of Energy" but not "Energetic Systems Agency". Substring matches inside words are deliberately not used.
- OQ-73 (§6 stage 2 code match / geography unknowns) SPEC 6 gives "missing data = 0.5" only for eligibility; the same rule is applied where the NOTICE lacks the data: a notice with no code in any scheme scores code_match 0.5 ("notice lists no codes", common on Indian portals), an unknown place of performance scores geography 0.5, an unknown buyer scores buyer affinity 0.5 and an unknown estimated value scores value fit 0.5. A PROFILE that lacks the data is the owner's gap and scores 0 (no codes) or 1 (no state/city targets = whole already-filtered country; no value range = 0.5 since "no preference"). Value fit uses the INR range against INR notices when the profile has one, else the USD copies (core.money FX). Per-signal weighted values are rounded to cents before summing, so the breakdown always adds up to the stored score.
- OQ-74 (§6 / §5.3 US eligibility keys) No US requirements extractor exists yet (M5), so the US path reads a tolerant key set from `opportunity.eligibility`: `requires_small_business` (bool; when absent it is DERIVED from a small-business or socio-economic set-aside code), `min_avg_receipts_usd` / `min_annual_revenue_usd` (number or "$5M"), `min_experience_years`, `required_certifications` (names normalised like core.eligibility_in: "ISO 9001" == iso_9001), `required_registrations` (sam -> company_profiles.sam_status/sam_expires_on, falling back to registrations rows; other kinds -> registrations rows). The M5 extractor should emit these keys (or `core/matching/eligibility_signal.CriteriaUS.from_dict` gains the aliases). The IN path takes `due_on` from `response_due_at` when the extracted criteria omit it, so a DSC that lapses before the bid due date fails as OQ-23 intends; EMD stays informational and is excluded from the average.
- OQ-80 (§8 / §9 pursuits before M6-01) The agent pipeline needs a pursuit row to hang requirements, artifacts and cost caps on, but pursuits/stages/actions are M6-01. Decision: migration 0007_m5_agents creates a MINIMAL `pursuits` table (profile_id, opportunity_id, stage text default 'identified', owner_user_id, decision, internal_due_at, created_by, cost_cap_usd, unique (profile_id, opportunity_id)) with `services/pursuits.get_or_create` and an idempotent `POST /api/v1/pursuits {profile_id, opportunity_id}` (201 created / 200 existing, owner + bid_manager) so the workspace can be exercised now; M6-01 adds the stage enum/rules, key dates and `POST /opportunities/{id}/pursue|watch|pass` on top (it should route pursue through get_or_create). agent_runs.pursuit_id now carries a real FK (SET NULL).
- OQ-81 (§8 cost guard units) plan_limits.limit_value is an integer, so the tenant monthly LLM budget is `agent_budget_usd_month` in WHOLE USD (free 0, pro 50, enterprise NULL = unlimited; internal tenants unlimited via PlanService). The per-pursuit cap is money: `tenants.pursuit_cost_cap_usd numeric(12,2)` default 15 and `pursuits.cost_cap_usd` (NULL = tenant default), raised by approve-budget; the raise is recorded in audit_log (action pursuit.budget_approved, meta additional/previous/new cap, reason, run id) rather than a separate approvals table. Projection is deliberately rough (chars/4 tokens x configured price + an output allowance) and only stops a step BEFORE it spends; the ledger stays authoritative. A step without an estimator (collector) is free, but a run is still stopped when the month is already over budget and the step projects > 0.
- OQ-82 (§10.1 running the pipeline) `POST .../approve-budget` (and the coming `POST .../agents/run`) queue Celery task `bidradar.run_agents(run_id, tenant_id)` and fall back to running the job in the API process when no broker answers or `inline: true` is sent; when CELERY_TASK_ALWAYS_EAGER is on they also run in-process directly, because an eager task would call asyncio.run inside the API's event loop. The API process resolves the LLM from `app.state.llm` (llm_from_settings; tests inject FakeLLM) and storage/scanner from app.state, so an in-process run shares the API's fakes.

- OQ-83 (CLAUDE.md migration numbering after the main merge) main's head is now 0009_m7_billing_privacy (0004 -> 0005_m1_knowledge_base -> 0009), so 0007_m5_agents.down_revision was re-pointed from 0004 to 0009 to keep a single alembic head in this worktree. The revision id and file name stay 0007_m5_agents (M5-02..M5-14 edit it in place), so the chain reads 0005 -> 0009 -> 0007 out of numeric order until the orchestrator renumbers it at merge time along with the M3/M4 worktrees' 0006/0008.

- OQ-84 (§13.6 / M7-12 what the README is allowed to claim) SPEC 13.6 asks for "a README with setup, env vars, adapter guide and runbook for a broken source", written at a point where 33 of 112 tasks are still todo. Decision: the README documents what EXISTS and names what does not, with a "What works today" section derived from tasks.json counts and an explicit "Not yet built" list (matching signals and the stage-3 rationale, the event router, agents 4/5/6/8, exports, all of M6, most of the frontend, load/Playwright/acceptance). The env table is generated from `Settings.model_fields` (purpose and required-in-prod hand-written, default and secret flag read from config.py + SECRET_SETTINGS) and `tests/unit/test_docs_links.py` fails if a field is missing, unknown, unannotated, or if the secret column drifts from SECRET_SETTINGS — so the table cannot silently rot. The counts in the status section WILL go stale while M4/M5/M6 run in parallel worktrees; the section says so and points at tasks.json as the truth rather than being regenerated on every merge.

- OQ-85 (§5.1 / M7-12 `sources.enabled` is reported but not honoured) Writing the broken-source runbook surfaced a real gap: `sources.enabled` is set from the registry by `sync_sources` and preserved across syncs (on_conflict updates only region/schedule), and it is returned by `GET /api/v1/admin/sources` and `/admin/health` — but NOTHING reads it. `build_beat_schedule`, `app/jobs/run_source.default_adapter_factory` and `app/jobs/smoke` all ask `registry.is_enabled(cls)`. An operator who flips the column to stop a misbehaving portal therefore changes a display value and nothing else. The runbook documents the two switches that DO work (pause the per-adapter Cloud Scheduler trigger; `enabled = False` in the registry or gepnic_configs.yaml plus a deploy) and flags the column as a known gap. Wiring it — the runner and the beat schedule reading the row, with the registry as the default — belongs with the admin-console work, not with a docs task.

- OQ-86 (§5.1/§7 / M7-12 nothing pages anyone when an adapter fails mid-day) `adapter.failing` is published by `source_runner` after more than two consecutive failing runs and has a notification template and category in `app/notify/render.py` ("source health alerts"), but no subscriber connects the event to a channel: the only path that actually reaches a human is the nightly-smoke workflow's `OPS_SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL`. So a source that breaks at 09:00 is noticed at 03:00 UTC the next night, up to ~18 hours later. Written down in the runbook rather than fixed here, because the subscriber belongs to M4-14 (event router). SPEC 5.1's "a nightly live smoke test per source alerts us" is satisfied; "pages the admin channel" in near-real-time is not.

- OQ-87 (§7 / M4-16 the preference route does not expose the email opt-outs) `user_notification_prefs.unsubscribed_categories` is written by the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe token route but is NOT in `NotificationPrefsOut`, and `NotificationPrefsIn` is `extra="forbid"`, so Settings > Notifications can neither read it from the API nor let the user re-subscribe. The page therefore renders the list READ-ONLY from `unsubscribed_categories` when a response happens to carry it (the e2e mock does) and says "None" otherwise. Backend change wanted: add the field to the GET response and accept it on PUT; the frontend already normalises and displays it (`src/lib/notifications/prefs.ts`).

- OQ-88 (§7 / M4-16 where the web-push VAPID public key comes from) The backend holds `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` in settings but publishes it nowhere (`GET /system/info` returns version, region, environment only), so the browser reads it from `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, inlined at build time — the same per-environment image problem as OQ-77. Without the variable the button is disabled and says "Web push is not configured for this deployment" rather than failing on click. Safer fix when someone touches either: return `vapid_public_key` from `/system/info` and read it at runtime.

- OQ-89 (§6 / M4-16 contracts assumed for the not-yet-merged M4-08 routes) Coded against, and mocked in e2e: `GET/POST /saved-searches {id, name, filters}` plus `PATCH`/`DELETE /saved-searches/{id}` for rename and delete (the brief named only GET/POST, but the settings page needs both); `GET/POST /alert-rules` and `PATCH /alert-rules/{id}` with {id, saved_search_id, profile_id, name, min_score, channels[], mode instant|digest, enabled}; `POST /opportunities/{id}/feedback {thumb up|down, reason}`. A saved search with no rule yet shows "No rule yet" and the first edit POSTs one with mode digest, min_score 70, channels ["email"]. Every one of these answers 404 today and the UI says so instead of pretending.

- OQ-90 (§10.4 screen 2 / M4-16 the /dashboard shape is read tolerantly) The M6 aggregate is documented as `pipeline_value_by_stage {USD, INR}`, which can be read as one total per currency or as a currency pair per stage. `src/lib/dashboard/api.ts` accepts BOTH (all-currency keys -> a single total; anything else -> per stage, summed for the headline) and also tolerates `usd`/`inr`, `name`/`title` and `response_due_at`/`due_at` in `due_next_7_days`. `win_rate` and `alert_precision` are read as fractions and rendered as percentages.

- OQ-91 (§6 / M4-16 `min_score` is accepted but not applied by the search route) `GET /opportunities?min_score=` is still a no-op server-side until matches exist (M2-15 left it that way), so Home's "High-fit today" card shows whatever the API returns — today the unscored notices with an em dash. The e2e mock mirrors exactly that: `min_score` filters only when the fixtures carry a `match` (the `matches` option), which is what the scored API will do.

- OQ-92 (§3 / M7-09 there is no tenant member endpoint at all) M0-07 provisions a membership just in time on the first authenticated call, so a tenant's members exist in the database but no route lists, invites, re-roles or removes them. Settings > Users and roles is therefore built against a contract — `GET /api/v1/tenant/members` (rows {id, user_id, email, name, role, status, invited_at, last_seen_at}), `POST /tenant/members/invite {email, role}`, `PATCH /tenant/members/{id} {role}`, `DELETE /tenant/members/{id}` — mocked in e2e and answering 404 on main, where the page says so. The orchestrator should assign the backend half; the owner-only rule and the "last owner keeps the role" guard are already in the UI but must be enforced server-side too.

- OQ-93 (§7 / M7-09 a WhatsApp BSP key cannot be pasted) `IntegrationIn` is `extra="forbid"` and declares only `webhook_url`, `signing_secret` and `secret_ref`, although `SECRET_FIELDS` in the same module lists `api_key`. So the WhatsApp card (and the two calendar cards) can only point at a platform-held secret (`env:NAME`, `sm://…`), which is what the UI asks for and explains. Adding `api_key` to `IntegrationIn` would let a tenant paste their Gupshup/Twilio key; until then the provider, sender and template names are plain `config` and the credential is a reference. Related: there is no send path for WhatsApp yet (`app/notify/` has slack, teams, email, push, no whatsapp), and no test-connection route for any kind, so the integration cards say that the first real alert is the test.

- OQ-94 (§3/§11 / M7-09 what the settings screens do NOT let you do) Downgrading to Free and cancelling a subscription have no route (checkout is upgrade-only and `PAID_PLANS` excludes free), so the billing page says to write to support; a plan only ever changes on a provider webhook. Tenant erasure is offered to the owner behind a typed confirmation, but there is no undo and no grace period on the API — the dialog says so rather than pretending to offer one. Invoice amounts are rendered as minor units / 100 for both Stripe and Razorpay, which holds for USD and INR.

- OQ-95 (§3 / M7-15 a member row has no "invited" state) The memberships table has no status, invited_at or last_seen_at column, so `GET /api/v1/tenant/members` returns `{id, user_id, email, name, role, joined_at}` — `id` is the membership id (PATCH/DELETE take it) and `joined_at` is `memberships.created_at`. The OQ-92 contract's `status` / `invited_at` / `last_seen_at` are therefore gone from the wire and Settings > Users and roles shows a "Joined" column instead of an invented "active" badge; the e2e fixtures and mock now mirror the real shape. An invitee who has never signed in is indistinguishable from an active member until someone adds `last_seen_at` (or an invitations table with an expiring accept token).

- OQ-96 (§3 / M7-15 a role change only takes effect at the next token refresh) `PATCH /tenant/members/{id}` writes `memberships.role`, but RBAC still reads the role claim from the Auth.js token (OQ-18), so a demoted user keeps their old permissions until their session token is refreshed and the API cannot revoke a session. The same applies to `DELETE`: the membership is gone, yet the bearer token still names the tenant, so that user's requests keep passing `get_current_user` (they simply see an empty tenant through RLS, and `ensure_user_membership` re-provisions a membership at their token's role on the next `/me` call — a removal is only durable once the identity provider stops issuing that tenant claim). Making the database authoritative means one indexed membership lookup per request in `get_current_user`.

- OQ-97 (§3/§7 / M7-15 the invitation email is fire-and-forget and carries no accept link) The invite dispatches a `member.invited` event through the notify Dispatcher inside the request (email channel only, `app/notify/templates/invite.*`), so a slow SMTP provider slows the POST, and a failed send leaves the membership in place with a `failed` delivery row rather than telling the owner. There is no signed invitation token because there is no signup route (OQ-60): the mail says "sign in with this address", which works because the magic link provisions the user just in time. `member.invited` was added to the unsubscribe categories (alongside the pre-existing `adapter_failing`, whose footer token had never verified) so the CAN-SPAM footer link on those two ops emails is valid; a transactional invite arguably should not carry one at all.

- OQ-90 (§6 keyword signal with no keywords) A profile with no include keywords has nothing to match on, so `keyword_match` is UNKNOWN (0.5, note "no include keywords") rather than 0. OQ-73 scores a profile that lacks data 0 for `code_match` because codes are the primary expression of what a company sells; keywords are optional enrichment on top of them, and zeroing a 10-weight signal would punish every profile that finished the wizard without typing keywords. Same for keywords that all carry weight 0.
- OQ-91 (§6 "keyword BM25 ... normalized 0-1") Postgres has no BM25; SPEC 6's acceptance line names `ts_rank_cd`, whose raw cover-density rank is unbounded (it grows with the number of occurrences). Each per-term rank is therefore normalised with Postgres's own flag 32 (`rank / (rank + 1)`) rather than by dividing by the rank of the profile's own keyword string against itself: flag 32 needs no reference document, is scale-free, and is stable when the owner edits their keyword list. One tsvector is built per notice from title (weight A), summary_ai + description (B) and the parsed document chunks (C, capped at 120k characters so the 1 MB tsvector limit can never be hit), so the calibration reads: one occurrence in the title = 0.50, one in the description = 0.29, one inside a document = 0.17, a term repeated throughout the notice -> 1. The profile's own `profile_keywords.weight` values are the COMBINATION weights (weighted average across terms), not tsquery weights, because a tsquery weight label selects document weights rather than scaling a term.
